 figures+='<section><img alt="'+key+'" src="data:image/png;base64,'+enc+'"></section>'
(O/'Fresh_Crater_Fit_Explorer_v5.html').write_text(html.replace('PAYLOAD',payload).replace('FIGURES',figures))

('Fresh crater evidence and a thick atmosphere hypothesis',[
"The fresh-crater analysis makes a dense early Martian atmosphere a serious hypothesis to test. Reproduction of the archived entry surrogate assigns 58.48% of its pressure-grid weight to 3 bar, compared with 6.76%, 6.81%, 10.04% and 17.91% at 0.5, 1, 1.5 and 2 bar. Adding the published +4.5 km elevation level changes the 3 bar weight to 64.03%. These are conditional weights within a finite exploratory grid, not measured pressure probabilities. Because 3 bar is the upper tested boundary, the calculation motivates extending the grid above 3 bar; it does not establish pressures above 3 bar.",
"The input distinction is essential. Archer (2023, Table 4) reports fresh crater depth or rim-to-floor relief within 2–3, 3–4 and 4–5 km diameter groups, not mean diameter as its response variable [A11]. All 27 means and their reported standard deviations are now included. Their elevation regressions give slopes of 5.14 ± 0.79, 3.27 ± 0.32 and 3.62 ± 0.19 m per km, with R² = 0.857, 0.937 and 0.981. The slope errors are ordinary-regression standard errors; the plotted error bars are individual-crater standard deviations. The independent deduplicated catalogue supplies actual diameter-versus-elevation statistics and counts, which are reported separately.",
"Good empirical elevation correlations do not validate the entry model. For the full 27-mean dataset, the best sampled 3 bar profile has pooled R² = 0.969 but within-size R² = −2.69, −2.67 and −0.10. The pooled value is dominated by differences among crater-size groups. The negative within-group values reveal poor predictive agreement with the smaller elevation trends. Fresh morphology also supplies no formation age. Thus these results cannot by themselves assign a 3 bar atmosphere to the Noachian."
]),
('Entry physics composition and uncertainty',[
"The archived surrogate models drag and a heuristic material-survival weight. It has no ablation or resolved fragmentation calculation. Its gas-constant mixture is also inconsistent with an interpretation of the reported gas fractions as mole fractions. A separate sensitivity run uses R = Rᵤ/ΣxᵢMᵢ, retaining the archived relief and survival assumptions. The 3 bar weight becomes 72.63% under the nominal likelihood but only 20.69% if the published standard deviations replace the assumed SD/√20 mean errors. The nominal effective sample size of 20 and the 20 m error floor are analyst assumptions. This sensitivity prevents the pressure ranking from being presented as a robust retrieval.",
"New single-body calculations explicitly track drag and ablation for 150, 250, 350 and 450 m projectiles over the pressure and CO₂/H₂/H₂O scenarios. A population sensitivity also provides predictive R² for each pressure, composition, humidity, heat-transfer coefficient and crater-size group. These are mechanistic sensitivity tests with fixed stony material, a declared size and velocity distribution and no fragmentation; they are not a calibrated replacement for the historical inversion. All scenario tables, predictions, observed values, error definitions and executable code accompany the fit report."
]),
('Cool wet environments and the source of atmospheric mass',[
"I therefore propose a sustained or episodic cool, wet Noachian Mars with a much denser atmosphere than today as a physically motivated working hypothesis. A thicker atmosphere containing appropriate greenhouse gases could support seasonal liquid water, precipitation, groundwater recharge and a wider range of connected surface and subsurface environments than a uniformly cold, dry endmember. Kamada et al. obtained a cool-and-wet climate near 1.5 bar with about 3% H₂ in their tested 0.5–2 bar range [A4]. Their result supports the climate mechanism; it is not a published 3 bar simulation. This is not evidence for survival of an unmodified primordial hydrogen–helium envelope. ('Impact resilience and the limits of sterilization claims',[
"Impact bombardment need not reset an entire biosphere. Abramov and Mojzsis found subsurface refuges in their modeled terrestrial late-heavy-bombardment cases, including extreme sensitivity cases, and their Mars calculations found limited crustal melting and enlarged hydrothermal habitable volumes across four bombardment histories [A1,A2]. Local destruction and the creation of new habitats can therefore occur together. These results strengthen the case for biological continuity where suitable refuges existed.",
"The latest verified Abramov paper, published in September 2026, addresses a different and earlier interval. It includes hot ejecta, rock-vapour condensation and evolving geothermal gradients, finding repeated global sterilization of modeled near-surface biocompatible environments before about 4.4 Ga and a favorable RNA-World window near 4.33 Ga [A3]. This prevents a categorical claim that no plausible early impact history could completely sterilize a planet. The result concerns persistence of biochemistry under specified thermal assumptions, not a measurement of the age or location of life's origin.",
"Venus remains a conditional extension: a wet crust could in principle contain impact refuges, but the cited Earth and Mars calculations do not demonstrate Venusian survival. ('A testable Martian nursery and a highland sampling strategy',[
"The Martian nursery hypothesis remains viable as a research question rather than a demonstrated origin history. Diverse terrain, mineral surfaces, wet–dry cycles and redox or thermal gradients could concentrate precursors, promote reactions and preserve chemical products. Rover evidence for repeated wet–dry cycling and recent evaporite modeling provide concrete mechanisms worth testing [A6,A12]. A cooler, spatially varied Mars could have offered advantages over a hypothetical hotter, ocean-dominated Earth or Venus. That comparison is conditional: early Earth also had concentration mechanisms and evidence for exposed land, and neither terrestrial nor Venusian oceans can be assumed to have been uniformly mixed. No measured global ranking of prebiotic productivity follows from this model.",
"A decisive program should pair lowland sedimentary samples with independently dated Noachian highland bedrock, alteration zones, paleosols or evaporites, and impact-related hydrothermal materials. Highlands preserve some of the oldest extensive exposed crustal records on the terrestrial planets, while Martian meteorites contain roughly 4.45 Ga crustal minerals [A7]. Noachian terrain age is not the crystallization age of every rock, and highlands should not be called unconditionally the oldest known crustal material.",
"Such samples could test whether atmospheric retention, aqueous chemistry and precursor concentration coexisted early enough to make Mars advantageous, and whether apparently biological signals survive independent tests. from pathlib import Path
from collections import defaultdict
import csv,hashlib,struct,zipfile
root=Path('/mnt/data'); out=root/'archer_2023_combined_analysis';out.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
files=sorted(root.glob('*.shx')); rows=[]; byhash=defaultdict(list)
for f in files:
 b=f.read_bytes(); digest=hashlib.sha256(b).hexdigest();byhash[digest].append(f.name)
 valid=len(b)>=100 and struct.unpack('>i',b[0:4])[0]==9994 and (len(b)-100)%8==0
 n=(len(b)-100)//8 if valid else None
 name=f.stem
 # match exact names, strip Windows duplicate suffixes and Copy, preserve A distinction
 import re
 base=re.sub(r'\(\d+\)$','',name);base=re.sub(r' - Copy$','',base)
 matches=[x for x in root.glob('*.dbf') if x.stem.lower()==base.lower()]
 if not matches and base=='InvVNN0p5shp':matches=list(root.glob('InvVNN0p5shp*.dbf'))
 dbf=matches[0] if matches else None
 dbf_n=struct.unpack('<I',dbf.read_bytes()[4:8])[0] if dbf else None
 rows.append(dict(shx_file=f.name,sha256=digest,shx_record_count=n,valid_shx_header=valid,canonical_stem=base,matched_dbf=dbf.name if dbf else '',dbf_record_count=dbf_n,record_count_agrees=(n==dbf_n if dbf else ''),shp_present=bool(list(root.glob(base+'.shp'))),duplicate_shx_copies=''))
for r in rows:r['duplicate_shx_copies']='; '.join(x for x in byhash[r['sha256']] if x!=r['shx_file'])
with (out/'spatial_component_audit.csv').open('w',newline='') as f:
 w=csv.DictWriter(f,fieldnames=rows[0].keys());w.writeheader();w.writerows(rows)
with (out/'SPATIAL_DATA_README.txt').open('w') as f:
 f.write('ARCHER (2023) UPDATED SPATIAL COMPONENT AUDIT\n\n')
 f.write(f'Uploaded: {len(list(root.glob("*.dbf")))} DBF, {len(files)} SHX, {len(list(root.glob("*.shp")))} SHP.\n')
 f.write(f'SHX distinct file hashes: {len(byhash)}; repeated copies: {len(files)-len(byhash)}.\n')
 f.write(f'SHX matching DBF record counts: {sum(r["record_count_agrees"] is True for r in rows)} / {sum(bool(r["matched_dbf"]) for r in rows)}.\n')
 f.write('IMPORTANT: SHX is an index, not spatial geometry. No .shp files were uploaded.\n')
 f.write('Consequently no geospatial reconstruction, accurate channel geometry/lengths, areal densities, or spatially explicit GCM validation can be derived from these SHX files.\n')
 f.write('Prior cleaned CSVs and plots are first-pass DBF-based exploratory outputs, NOT final climate hypothesis testing.\n')
 f.write('Original input DBF data were not modified. SHX duplicates were identified by SHA-256, not merged into crater records.\n')
 f.write('For a spatial analysis, provide matching .shp files (and .prj files when available) plus per-elevation Noachian terrain areas.\n')
 f.write('The Python script build_archer_analysis.py is retained to reproduce the first-pass analysis.\n')
archive=root/'archer_2023_combined_results_updated.zip'
with zipfile.ZipFile(archive,'w',zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED) as z:
 for p in sorted(out.glob('*')):z.write(p,arcname=p.name)
 for p in [root/'build_archer_analysis.py',root/'audit_spatial_components.py']:z.write(p,arcname=p.name)
print('shx',len(files),'unique',len(byhash),'matched',sum(bool(r['matched_dbf']) for r in rows),'count agree',sum(r['record_count_agrees'] is True for r in rows),'zip',archive,archive.stat().st_size)
print('mismatches',[(r['shx_file'],r['shx_record_count'],r['dbf_record_count']) for r in rows if r['matched_dbf'] and not r['record_count_agrees']][:20])


================================================================================
research/crater_atmosphere/Fresh_Crater_Atmosphere_and_Impact_Revision/original_inputs/archer_2023_combined_results_updated/build_archer_analysis.py

import os,glob,re,json,zipfile,hashlib
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np,pandas as pd,shapefile
from scipy import stats
import matplotlib; matplotlib.use('Agg')
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
ROOT=Path('/mnt/data'); OUT=ROOT/'archer_2023_combined_analysis';OUT.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
files=sorted(ROOT.glob('*.dbf'))
cr=[]; inv=[]; audit=[]
for f in files:
 r=shapefile.Reader(dbf=str(f),encoding='latin1'); cols=[a[0] for a in r.fields[1:]]
 typ='inverted_channel' if f.name.startswith('InvVN') else ('fresh' if 'Fresh' in f.name else 'degraded')
 mm=re.search(r'(?:Neg|UP|InvVNN|InvVNU)(\d)p5',f.name)
 mid=(int(mm.group(1))+.5)*(-1 if f.name.startswith(('Neg','InvVNN')) else 1) if mm else np.nan
 count=0
 for i,record in enumerate(r.iterRecords()):
  d=dict(zip(cols,record)); d.update(source_file=f.name,source_row=i+1,file_class=typ,file_bin_mid_km=mid)
  (inv if typ=='inverted_channel' else cr).append(d);count+=1
 audit.append(dict(file=f.name,type=typ,bin_center_km=mid,records=count,fields=';'.join(cols)))
pd.DataFrame(audit).to_csv(OUT/'input_inventory.csv',index=False)
c=pd.DataFrame(cr);v=pd.DataFrame(inv)
for x in ['DIAM_CIRCL','DEPTH_RIMF','DEGRADATIO','LATITUDE_C','LONGITUDE_','OBJECTID']:
 c[x]=pd.to_numeric(c[x],errors='coerce')
c['crater_id_key']=c['CRATER_ID'].astype(str).str.strip()
c['class_from_grade']=np.select([c.DEGRADATIO.isin([1,2]),c.DEGRADATIO.isin([3,4])],['degraded','fresh'],default='unknown')
c['file_grade_disagreement']=c.file_class.ne(c.class_from_grade)
c['diameter_bin']=pd.cut(c.DIAM_CIRCL,[2,3,4,5],right=False,labels=['2-3','3-4','4-5'])
c['in_scope_2_5km']=c.DIAM_CIRCL.ge(2)&c.DIAM_CIRCL.lt(5)
# Preserve original files as converted CSV and full audit
for f,grp in c.groupby('source_file',sort=True):grp.to_csv(OUT/(f+'.csv'),index=False)
for f,grp in v.groupby('source_file',sort=True):grp.to_csv(OUT/(f+'.csv'),index=False)
# Dedup on stable crater id, separately log conflicts. Do not collapse classification contradictions silently.
c['key']=np.where(c.crater_id_key.str.len()>2,c.crater_id_key,'loc_'+c.LATITUDE_C.round(3).astype(str)+'_'+c['LONGITUDE_'].round(3).astype(str)+'_'+c.DIAM_CIRCL.round(2).astype(str))
c['key_occurrences']=c.groupby('key')['key'].transform('size')
c['key_class_count']=c.groupby('key')['class_from_grade'].transform('nunique')
c['grade_priority']=np.where(c.class_from_grade.eq(c.file_class),0,1)
c=c.sort_values(['key','grade_priority','source_file','source_row'])
duplicates=c[c.duplicated('key',keep='first')].copy(); duplicates.to_csv(OUT/'removed_duplicate_crater_rows.csv',index=False)
conflicts=c[c.key_class_count>1].copy();conflicts.to_csv(OUT/'crater_classification_conflicts.csv',index=False)
unique=c.drop_duplicates('key',keep='first').copy()
unique['analysis_class']=np.where(unique.key_class_count>1,'conflicted',unique.class_from_grade)
unique.to_csv(OUT/'all_craters_deduplicated.csv',index=False)
unique[unique.analysis_class=='fresh'].to_csv(OUT/'fresh_craters_clean.csv',index=False)
unique[unique.analysis_class=='degraded'].to_csv(OUT/'degraded_craters_clean.csv',index=False)
# Inverted channel Id is NOT a unique key. Geometry is unavailable in DBF-only upload.
for x in ['Length','Z_Mean','Latitude','Longitude']:v[x]=pd.to_numeric(v[x],errors='coerce')
v['inferred_elevation_km']=v.Z_Mean/1000
v['actual_bin_mid_km']=np.floor(v.inferred_elevation_km)+0.5
v['bin_mismatch']=~np.isclose(v.actual_bin_mid_km,v.file_bin_mid_km)
# exact geometry-attribute duplicate only, retain separate features with identical centroid if length differs
v['fingerprint']=v[['Length','Z_Mean','Latitude','Longitude','Morphology']].astype(str).agg('|'.join,axis=1)
v=v.sort_values(['source_file','source_row'])
v[v.duplicated('fingerprint')].to_csv(OUT/'removed_duplicate_channel_rows.csv',index=False)
v=v.drop_duplicates('fingerprint')
v.to_csv(OUT/'inverted_channels_deduplicated.csv',index=False)
# Aggregate. In absence of AOI area per elevation interval, fractions NOT spatial densities.
a=unique[unique.analysis_class.isin(['fresh','degraded']) & unique.in_scope_2_5km].copy()
counts=a.groupby(['file_bin_mid_km','diameter_bin','analysis_class'],observed=True).size().unstack(fill_value=0).reset_index()
for cls in ['fresh','degraded']:
 if cls not in counts:counts[cls]=0
counts['total']=counts.fresh+counts.degraded
counts['fresh_fraction']=counts.fresh/counts.total
counts['degraded_to_fresh']=counts.degraded/counts.fresh.replace(0,np.nan)
counts.to_csv(OUT/'crater_elevation_diameter_counts.csv',index=False)
depths=a.groupby(['file_bin_mid_km','diameter_bin','analysis_class'],observed=True).agg(n=('DEPTH_RIMF','count'),mean_depth_km=('DEPTH_RIMF','mean'),sd_depth_km=('DEPTH_RIMF','std'),mean_diameter_km=('DIAM_CIRCL','mean')).reset_index()
depths['sem_depth_km']=depths.sd_depth_km/np.sqrt(depths.n)
depths.to_csv(OUT/'crater_depth_statistics.csv',index=False)
chan=v.groupby('file_bin_mid_km').agg(n=('Length','size'),sum_length_field=('Length','sum'),mean_length_field=('Length','mean'),mean_actual_elev_km=('inferred_elevation_km','mean'),bin_mismatch=('bin_mismatch','sum')).reset_index()
chan.to_csv(OUT/'inverted_channel_bin_statistics.csv',index=False)
# Published Archer Appendix A Table 2 coefficients, in km. x = median elevation in km.
published={
 'fresh_2-3':[.20883,.003521], 'fresh_3-4':[.36905,.0032369], 'fresh_4-5':[.47525,.0035797],
 'degraded_2-3':[.13617,.016771,.0030185,-.0024544], 'degraded_3-4':[.17843,.019265,-8.7861e-6,-.0053494], 'degraded_4-5':[.20872,.010936,.0039197,-.0049133],
 'difference_2-3':[.061556,-.016058,-.0014475,-.0029429], 'difference_3-4':[.19947,.01059852,-.0022454,.0026611], 'difference_4-5':[.2715,.0034564,-.0052407,.0035734],
 'VN_below_0p5':[.027861,.010009], 'VN_above_0p5':[.036326,-.0045988], 'VN_plus_inverted_below_0p5':[.028787,.0095978],
 'inverted_all':[.00081826,-.0005588,.00020233,-3.456e-5,-5.9573e-5],
 'deg_fresh_ratio_vs_VN_below_neg0p5':[2.0544,-39.182], 'deg_fresh_ratio_vs_VN_above_neg0p5':[.6224,17.554]
}
pd.DataFrame([{'relation':k,'coefficients_low_to_high':json.dumps(val)} for k,val in published.items()]).to_csv(OUT/'archer_published_equations.csv',index=False)
# Compare observed mean depths with published regressions, WITHOUT claiming exact reproduction of original table
rows=[]
for _,row in depths.iterrows():
 key=f"{row.analysis_class}_{row.diameter_bin}"
 if key not in published:continue
 x=row.file_bin_mid_km;co=published[key];pred=sum(t*x**j for j,t in enumerate(co))
 rows.append(dict(elevation_mid_km=x,diameter_bin=row.diameter_bin,crater_class=row.analysis_class,n=row.n,observed_depth_km=row.mean_depth_km,published_fit_km=pred,residual_km=row.mean_depth_km-pred))
compare=pd.DataFrame(rows);compare.to_csv(OUT/'published_vs_uploaded_depths.csv',index=False)
# Conditional odds and regression; NOT Bayesian climate hypothesis posterior.
reg=[]
for diam,group in counts.groupby('diameter_bin',observed=True):
 g=group[(group.total>0)&(group.file_bin_mid_km.between(-3.5,3.5))]
 if len(g)>2:
  # binomial GLM using statsmodels when available
  try:
   import statsmodels.api as sm
   fit=sm.GLM(g.fresh_fraction,sm.add_constant(g.file_bin_mid_km),family=sm.families.Binomial(),freq_weights=g.total).fit()
   reg.append(dict(diameter_bin=diam,log_odds_per_km=fit.params.iloc[1],odds_ratio_per_km=np.exp(fit.params.iloc[1]),p_value=fit.pvalues.iloc[1],n=int(g.total.sum()),model='binomial_glm_no_area_offset'))
  except Exception as e:reg.append(dict(diameter_bin=diam,error=str(e)))
pd.DataFrame(reg).to_csv(OUT/'fresh_preservation_elevation_glm.csv',index=False)
# physical sensitivity table: pressure, hydrogen, temperature, scale height
sens=[]
for p in [.5,1,1.5,2,3]:
 for h in [0,.05,.10,.15,.20,.25]:
  mm=(1-h)*44.01+h*2.016
  for T in [230,250,273,283]:
   H=8.314462618*T/(mm/1000*3.71)/1000
   sens.append(dict(surface_pressure_bar=p,h2_mole_fraction=h,co2_mole_fraction=1-h,T_K=T,mean_molar_mass_g_mol=mm,scale_height_km=H,p_at_5km_bar=p*np.exp(-5/H),p_at_10km_bar=p*np.exp(-10/H)))
pd.DataFrame(sens).to_csv(OUT/'atmosphere_scale_height_sensitivity.csv',index=False)
# plots
fig,ax=plt.subplots(figsize=(9,5))
for diam,g in counts.groupby('diameter_bin',observed=True):ax.plot(g.file_bin_mid_km,g.fresh_fraction,'o-',label=str(diam)+' km')
ax.axvline(1,color='grey',ls='--',label='Proposed LNIH ELA');ax.set(xlabel='Elevation bin center (km)',ylabel='Fresh fraction among classified 2–5 km craters',title='Crater preservation by elevation and diameter');ax.legend();fig.tight_layout();fig.savefig(OUT/'crater_fresh_fraction.png',dpi=160);plt.close(fig)
fig,ax=plt.subplots(figsize=(9,5))
for (cls,diam),g in depths.groupby(['analysis_class','diameter_bin'],observed=True):
 if len(g):ax.plot(g.file_bin_mid_km,g.mean_depth_km,'o-',label=f'{cls} {diam} km')
ax.axvline(1,color='grey',ls='--');ax.set(xlabel='Elevation bin center (km)',ylabel='Mean rim-floor relief (km)',title='Uploaded crater relief by elevation');ax.legend(fontsize=8,ncol=2);fig.tight_layout();fig.savefig(OUT/'crater_relief.png',dpi=160);plt.close(fig)
fig,ax=plt.subplots(figsize=(9,5));ax.bar(chan.file_bin_mid_km,chan.n,width=.7);ax.set(xlabel='File elevation bin center (km)',ylabel='Inverted channel DBF record count',title='Inverted-channel record counts (not areal densities)');fig.tight_layout();fig.savefig(OUT/'inverted_channel_counts.png',dpi=160);plt.close(fig)
summary=dict(input_files=len(files),input_crater_rows=len(c),unique_craters=len(unique),removed_crater_duplicates=len(duplicates),conflicted_unique_craters=int((unique.analysis_class=='conflicted').sum()),file_grade_disagreement_rows=int(c.file_grade_disagreement.sum()),unique_fresh=int((unique.analysis_class=='fresh').sum()),unique_degraded=int((unique.analysis_class=='degraded').sum()),input_inverted_channel_rows=len(inv),unique_inverted_channel_rows=len(v),inverted_channel_bin_mismatch=int(v.bin_mismatch.sum()),crater_analysis_rows_2_to_5km=len(a),note='DBF files have attributes only; spatial geometries and bin-specific terrain areas not provided. No actual 3D GCM simulation or calibrated Bayesian climate posterior performed.')
(OUT/'analysis_summary.json').write_text(json.dumps(summary,indent=2))
(OUT/'README.txt').write_text('ARCHER 2023 COUPLED ANALYSIS - REPRODUCIBLE ATTRIBUTES AND DIAGNOSTICS\n\n'+json.dumps(summary,indent=2)+'\n\nMethods: exact crater ID deduplication; class derived from DEGRADATIO (1/2 degraded, 3/4 fresh); conflicting class excluded from analysis, retained in master CSV; duplicated channel records identified by identical length, Z_Mean, lat, lon, morphology. Bin elevation from filename, and actual inverted-channel Z_Mean also retained. The source files lack shapefile .shp geometry and elevation-bin terrain areas; thus no physically justified VN length per area or crater density per area. Fresh/degraded ratios are valid within matched sampled bins if samples share selection masks. Published coefficients transcribed from Archer (2023) Appendix A Table 2; regressions not refit to figures. Negative/positive upper bins may overlap by user-provided definitions, inspect bin mismatch audit. Model atmosphere scale heights are ideal-gas diagnostics only, not climate simulations. No calibration of erosion K, no net water balance, no likelihood-based climate hypothesis ranking without GCM scenario outputs.\n')
with zipfile.ZipFile(ROOT/'archer_2023_combined_results.zip','w',compression=zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED,compresslevel=6) as z:
 for f in OUT.iterdir():z.write(f,arcname=f.name)
print(json.dumps(summary,indent=2));print('OUTPUTS',len(list(OUT.iterdir())))

import os, numpy as np, matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from reportlab.platypus import SimpleDocTemplate,Paragraph,Spacer,Table,TableStyle,PageBreak
from reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet,ParagraphStyle
from reportlab.lib import colors
from reportlab.lib.enums import TA_CENTER
p='/mnt/data/mars_submission_work';plt.rcParams.update({'font.family':'DejaVu Sans','font.size':10,'svg.fonttype':'none','axes.spines.top':False,'axes.spines.right':False,'savefig.bbox':'tight'})
fig,ax=plt.subplots(figsize=(7.2,4.2)); ax.axhspan(5.4,8,color='#b75a42',alpha=.14,label='Sun et al. (2025): candidate water-bearing layer');ax.axhline(5.4,color='#b75a42',lw=1,ls='--');ax.axhline(8,color='#b75a42',lw=1,ls='--');ax.set_ylim(10,0);ax.set_xlim(0,1);ax.set_xticks([]);ax.set_ylabel('Depth beneath Martian surface (km)');ax.set_title('Seismic constraint on a possible Martian aquifer',loc='left',weight='bold');ax.legend(loc='lower center',frameon=False,fontsize=8);fig.savefig(p+'/figure_01_seismic_depth.svg');fig.savefig(p+'/figure_01_seismic_depth.pdf');plt.close(fig)
fig,ax=plt.subplots(figsize=(7.2,4.2));ax.barh(['Estimated storage','Unaccounted inventory'],[780-520,920-710],left=[520,710],height=.45,color=['#b75a42','#4b8496']);ax.plot([520,780],[0,0],alpha=0);ax.set_xlim(0,1050);ax.set_xlabel('Global equivalent layer (m GEL)');ax.set_title('Published Martian water-volume ranges',loc='left',weight='bold');ax.text(650,0,'520–780',ha='center',va='center',color='white',weight='bold');ax.text(815,1,'710–920',ha='center',va='center',color='white',weight='bold');ax.text(10,1.65,'Ranges are literature bounds, not mean ± 1 SD.',fontsize=8);fig.savefig(p+'/figure_02_water_ranges.svg');fig.savefig(p+'/figure_02_water_ranges.pdf');plt.close(fig)
styles=getSampleStyleSheet();styles.add(ParagraphStyle(name='MainTitle2',parent=styles['Title'],fontSize=16,leading=20,spaceAfter=14));styles.add(ParagraphStyle(name='Body2',parent=styles['BodyText'],leading=15,spaceAfter=9));styles.add(ParagraphStyle(name='Small2',parent=styles['BodyText'],fontSize=8,leading=11))
refs=['Archer, R. (2023). The spatial distributions of degraded craters and valley networks on Mars as a function of elevation: Their implications for Noachian climate. Icarus 406, 115734. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.icarus.2023.115734','Sun, W., Tkalcic, H., Malusa, M. G. & Pan, Y. (2025). Seismic evidence of liquid water at the base of Mars’ upper crust. National Science Review 12, nwaf166. https://doi.org/10.1093/nsr/nwaf166','Kamada et al. (2020, 2021). Early Mars global climate simulations cited by Archer (2023). Full bibliographic metadata and exact numerical parameterizations require verification against the original papers.','Wright, V., Morzfeld, M. & Manga, M. (2024). Liquid water in the Martian mid-crust. Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences 121, e2409983121.']
base='''Archer (2023) examined 11,422,066 square kilometres of Noachian terrain, evaluating crater preservation, crater relief, valley networks and inverted channels as a function of elevation. The results do not indicate preferential preservation of fresh small craters above the proposed 1 km icy-highlands equilibrium line, and support substantial Noachian surface runoff and erosion. These are spatial and geomorphological constraints; they do not independently quantify globally habitable crustal volume. Sun et al. (2025) identify a shear-wave low-velocity interval from 5.4 to 8 km beneath InSight and interpret it as potentially water-saturated. Under the assumptions of 20–30% porosity, full saturation and global representativeness, the inferred reservoir corresponds to 520–780 m global equivalent layer. This remains an interpretation of seismic observations rather than a direct detection of globally distributed liquid water.'''
method='''For a planet p at geological time t, define H_p(t)=4*pi*R_p^2 * integral from z=0 to z=zmax of phi_p(z,t)*S_p(z,t)*I[Tmin<=T_p(z,t)<=Tmax]*I[chemical energy available] dz. Here phi is connected porosity, S liquid saturation, and I denotes an indicator. A geometric crustal-volume percentage requires a separate denominator and must not be conflated with pore-weighted liquid-water volume. For a uniform thin shell, GEL_p(t)=integral phi_p(z,t)*S_p(z,t) dz, adjusted for the geographic fraction of the planet supporting the reservoir. Thermal profiles may be approximated by T(z,t)=Tsurface(t)+q(t)*z/k(t) only when the conductive approximation is valid. Mean rates over 250 Myr are [z(t+0.25 Ga)-z(t)]/0.25 Ga, with sign convention stated. For N defensible parameter draws, the sample SD is sqrt(sum_i (x_i-mean(x))^2/(N-1)); shaded bands must reflect computed draws, not publication ranges.'''
limits='''The archived Mars–Earth time-series files were located in the project Library, but raw-byte extraction was not authorized in this session. Accordingly, no new time-dependent curves, 250-Myr rates, standard deviations, posterior probabilities, or comparative Mars/Earth numerical conclusions are represented here as calculated. Kamada model coefficients and Archer figure-level numerical tables must be checked against full originals before scientific submission. Shared genetic machinery among terrestrial life does not establish Martian origin. The likelihood of Mars-to-Earth biotic transport must include impact ejection, transit survival, entry, ecological establishment, and competing terrestrial-origin likelihoods; an uncalibrated Bayes factor is not evidence.'''
variants={
'Nature':('A changing subsurface frontier for habitability on Mars and Earth','The contrasting histories of surface hydrology and crustal thermal evolution on Mars and Earth motivate a testable model of the spatial redistribution of habitable water. Martian crater and valley-network distributions document ancient surface-water activity, while recent seismology permits, but does not establish globally, a present-day liquid-water reservoir at 5.4–8 km. A robust quantitative comparison requires jointly constrained thermal histories, connected porosity, saturation and energy supply. We present the observational basis and the forward-model framework, while identifying the unresolved evidence needed to test whether early Mars offered a larger habitable domain than Earth.'),
'Science':('Contrasting pathways of crustal habitability on early Mars and Earth','A central question in planetary habitability is whether water-rich environments persisted and migrated into the subsurface at different rates on Mars and Earth. Noachian geomorphology supports widespread Martian runoff and erosion, whereas InSight seismic inversions reveal a candidate present-day water-bearing horizon. The combined observations motivate a comparative time-dependent model, but do not alone establish its magnitude or the relative probabilities of biogenesis on either planet.'),
'National_Science_Review':('Thermal evolution, water sequestration and the changing habitable crust of Mars','The coupling between planetary heat loss, permeability and water availability may control the depth and extent of habitable crustal environments. We integrate published Noachian geomorphic constraints with a 2025 Martian seismic interpretation of a possible 5.4–8 km water-bearing layer. The resulting analytical framework specifies how depth-resolved habitability, global equivalent water layer and uncertainty should be calculated, and identifies necessary tests of lithopanspermia hypotheses.'),
'Icarus':('A quantitative framework for divergent crustal habitability on Mars and Earth','We formulate a depth-integrated water-weighted model of planetary crustal habitability constrained by crater degradation, valley networks, conductive thermal histories and present-day Martian seismic structure. The model distinguishes the physically habitable temperature window from accessible liquid-water pore volume and from biological occupancy. Reported contemporary Martian water storage is conditional on porosity, saturation and global extrapolation. Time-dependent numerical inference requires archived parameter ensembles and additional geophysical calibration.')}
for journal,(title,abstract) in variants.items():
 doc=SimpleDocTemplate(p+'/'+journal+'_research_draft.pdf',pagesize=(595,842),leftMargin=58,rightMargin=58,topMargin=55,bottomMargin=55)
 story=[Paragraph(title,styles['MainTitle2']),Paragraph('Research manuscript — source-grounded working draft; NOT ready for journal submission',styles['Small2']),Spacer(1,12),Paragraph('<b>Abstract</b>',styles['Heading2']),Paragraph(abstract,styles['Body2'])]
 for heading,txt in [('1. Introduction and observational basis',base),('2. Model definitions and mathematical derivation',method),('3. Interpreting the present-day reservoir','The 2.6 km layer thickness multiplied by 0.20–0.30 porosity gives 0.52–0.78 km of water equivalent, or 520–780 m GEL, if fully saturated and globally representative. These bounds are not standard deviations. They should not be plotted as ±1 SD.'),('4. Comparative habitability and panspermia','A larger early Martian habitable volume is a hypothesis, not yet an output validated from the available archive. A posterior probability of Mars-to-Earth transfer requires explicit competing hypotheses, priors and independent evidence likelihoods. An appropriate analysis will report sensitivity to these assumptions rather than treating possible transport as established ancestry.'),('5. Outstanding limitations and reproducibility',limits)]:story.extend([Paragraph(heading,styles['Heading2']),Paragraph(txt,styles['Body2'])])
 story.append(Paragraph('References (provisional; complete verification pending)',styles['Heading2']))
 for ref in refs:story.append(Paragraph(ref,styles['Small2']))
 doc.build(story)
abstracts={'LPSC':'Crustal water and thermal evolution may have redistributed Martian habitable environments from surface settings into subsurface refugia. Archer (2023) reports crater and valley-network patterns consistent with Noachian surface runoff and erosion across 11.42 million km² of Noachian terrain. Sun et al. (2025) infer a possible liquid-water-bearing horizon at 5.4–8 km from InSight seismic inversions. Under stated saturation and extrapolation assumptions, the reservoir corresponds to 520–780 m GEL. We outline a depth-integrated model for comparing these constraints with Earth, explicitly separating thermally permissive volume from water-bearing porosity and biological occupation.','AGU':'We develop a framework for propagating uncertainty in the long-term evolution of crustal liquid-water habitability on Mars and Earth. Noachian crater degradation and fluvial-network elevation distributions (Archer, 2023) constrain ancient hydrologic activity. A 5.4–8 km low-velocity layer beneath InSight (Sun et al., 2025) offers a potential modern reservoir constraint. A planned 250-Myr ensemble analysis will propagate thermal history, permeability and saturation uncertainty, reporting mean trajectories and sample standard deviations. Present findings are observational constraints and methodology, not yet calibrated global time-series results.','EGU':'The evolution of habitable planetary crust depends jointly on thermal gradients, pore connectivity, liquid-water saturation and chemical energy. We compare the geomorphic evidence for early Martian runoff reported by Archer (2023) with a contemporary seismic low-velocity interval at 5.4–8 km interpreted by Sun et al. (2025) as potentially water-saturated. We describe a coupled water-weighted depth integration and the statistical framework needed to compare Mars with Earth. The 520–780 m GEL estimate is conditional on 20–30% porosity and global extrapolation. We identify the parameter measurements needed to distinguish a progressively buried Martian refuge from alternative hydrologic histories.'}
for name,abstract in abstracts.items():
 doc=SimpleDocTemplate(p+'/'+name+'_abstract_draft.pdf',pagesize=(595,842));doc.build([Paragraph(name+' — Conference abstract (draft)',styles['MainTitle2']),Paragraph(abstract,styles['Body2']),Paragraph('Conference-year formatting and submission limits must be verified.',styles['Small2'])])
with open(p+'/README.txt','w') as f:f.write('SOURCE-GROUNDED PRELIMINARY PACKAGE, NOT SUBMISSION-READY.\nThe project folder was found, but raw materialization of its CSV and Python files failed with an authorization error. Consequently the package does not invent 250-Myr time-series values or standard deviations. Figures show published intervals, not model standard deviations. Four journal-specific draft PDFs and three conference abstract drafts are included.\n')
print(os.listdir(p))

p='/mnt/data/mars_submission_work';s=getSampleStyleSheet();s.add(ParagraphStyle(name='BodyA',parent=s['BodyText'],leading=15,spaceAfter=10));s.add(ParagraphStyle(name='SmallA',parent=s['BodyText'],fontSize=8,leading=11,spaceAfter=7))
sections=[('Archive integration and provenance','This supplement incorporates all archive findings that could be verified from accessible parsed project files. It does NOT claim complete incorporation of the entire Mars_NoachianHabitability folder: the Library permitted indexed reading, but denied raw-byte materialization of the model scripts and CSVs. Numerical reconstructions and uncertainty estimates therefore remain unverified.'),('1. Original planetary habitability model','The archived make_habitability.py implements 85 time points from 4.2 Ga to the present at 0.05-Ga spacing, with depths 0 to 15 km at 0.05-km spacing. It uses a tapered thermal suitability interval of 0 to 80 degrees C, water accessibility multipliers, and planet-specific pore closure depths of 10 km (Mars) and 4 km (Earth). This is an illustrative physical suitability model, not a measured biological habitat distribution.'),('2. Archived model equations','For Mars, assumed heat flow q_M=19+61(t/4.2)^1.35 mW/m2, conductivity k_M=2.5 W/(m K), and surface temperature T_s,M=-60+35(t/4.2) degrees C. For Earth, q_E=85+65(t/4.2)^1.1 mW/m2, k_E=3 W/(m K), and T_s,E=12+8(t/4.2) degrees C. The source script uses T=T_s+(q/k)z (consistent with q in mW/m2 and z in km). Mars assumed groundwater factor scales as 0.25+0.65(t/4.2), while Earth uses 0.8, further tapered near surface and pore closure. The global surface areas are 144.37 million km2 (Mars) and 510.1 million km2 (Earth). These assumptions must not be presented as measured geological histories.'),('3. These are scenario-weighted volumes and do not demonstrate that Mars had more total habitable cubic kilometers than Earth. The numerical trajectories require full CSV extraction and independent reproduction before inclusion in journal Results.'),('4. The impact-enabled visualization explicitly identifies its Gaussian 3.85-Ga bombardment interval as an assumed scenario, not a validated chronology, and 200–500 m impact heating as local rather than global. No-LHB is the preferred comparison baseline when avoiding a speculative bombardment maximum.'),('5. Interpretation of comparative percentage','The archived difference graph defines Mars minus Earth in percentage points of each planet’s own assumed pore-bearing depth domain, 10 km for Mars and 4 km for Earth. The archive also contains fresh/degraded crater deduplication and Bayesian preservation-test artifacts. Those archived outputs cannot be treated as independently validated numerical statistics until their underlying CSVs are accessible.'),('7. Sun et al. 2025 integration','Sun et al. (2025), National Science Review 12, nwaf166, interpret a low shear-wave velocity zone between 5.4 and 8 km beneath InSight as potentially water-saturated. Under 20–30% porosity, full saturation and global representativeness, 2.6 km thickness yields 520–780 m GEL. The authors discuss possible 710–920 m GEL unaccounted water inventory, and explicitly note lateral heterogeneity and incomplete saturation as uncertainties. These are ranges and assumptions, NOT measured one-standard-deviation error bars.'),('8. Kamada climate-model relationship','Archer (2023) cites Kamada et al. (2020, 2021) among models using CO2-H2 collision-induced absorption and denser early Martian atmospheres (including approximately 1.5 bar) as possible mechanisms for warmer Noachian climates. """Scenario-based, not uniquely calibrated, Tharsis-like volatile outgassing model.
Requires numpy pandas plotly matplotlib. Produces interactive HTML, PNG and CSV.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np,pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import plotly.graph_objects as go
from plotly.subplots import make_subplots
P=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
age=np.linspace(4,0,801);dt=.005;eu=(4.57/(4.57-age))**1.23;lum=1/(1+.4*age/4.57)
scenarios={'Literature-scale post-4Ga':(.35,22,.3,0),'Generous Tharsis':(1.5,65,1.,0),'Extreme 3-bar cumulative':(3.,130,2.,0),'Inherited 2.5-bar reservoir':(.35,22,.3,2.5)}

def run(name,co2_total,water_total,h2_water_ratio,initial):
 w=np.exp(-.5*((age-3.83)/.24)**2)+.25*np.exp(-.5*((age-3.27)/.4)**2)+.02*np.exp(-.5*((age-2.3)/.5)**2)
 w/=np.sum(w)*dt
 co2_source=co2_total*w; water_source=water_total*w
 h2_source=water_source*.0371*h2_water_ratio*(2/18)
 c=np.zeros(len(age));h=np.zeros(len(age));v=np.zeros(len(age));water=np.zeros(len(age)); c[0]=initial
 esc_c=np.zeros(len(age));esc_h=np.zeros(len(age));esc_w=np.zeros(len(age));fixed=np.zeros(len(age))
 for i in range(1,len(age)):
  j=i-1;shield=.65 if age[j]>3.7 else 1.
  loss_c=.022*eu[j]**.65*shield*c[j]; mineral=.024*c[j]
  tau=.012/np.sqrt(eu[j]/3)
  loss_h=h[j]/tau
  loss_w=.18*eu[j]**.7 if water[j]>0 else 0
  c[i]=max(0,c[j]+dt*(co2_source[j]-loss_c-mineral))
  h[i]=max(0,h[j]+dt*(h2_source[j]-loss_h))
  water[i]=max(0,water[j]+dt*(water_source[j]-loss_w))
  # Atmospheric vapor is saturation-limited; water inventory is distinct.
  temp=265+6*(lum[j]-.75)/.25
  saturation=.00611*np.exp(17.27*(temp-273.15)/(temp-35.85))
  v[i]=min(water[i]*.0371,.65*saturation)
  esc_c[i]=esc_c[j]+dt*loss_c;esc_h[i]=esc_h[j]+dt*loss_h;esc_w[i]=esc_w[j]+dt*loss_w;fixed[i]=fixed[j]+dt*mineral
 return pd.DataFrame(dict(scenario=name,age_Ga=age,CO2_atm_bar=c,H2_atm_bar=h,H2O_vapor_atm_bar=v,
  total_atm_bar=c+h+v,water_reservoir_GEL_m=water,CO2_outgassed_bar=np.cumsum(co2_source)*dt,
  H2_outgassed_bar=np.cumsum(h2_source)*dt,H2O_outgassed_GEL_m=np.cumsum(water_source)*dt,
  CO2_escaped_bar=esc_c,H2_escaped_bar=esc_h,H2O_lost_GEL_m=esc_w,CO2_sequestered_bar=fixed,
  CO2_source_bar_per_Gyr=co2_source,H2_source_bar_per_Gyr=h2_source,H2O_source_GEL_m_per_Gyr=water_source,
  solar_EUV_vs_today=eu,solar_luminosity_vs_today=lum))

frames={name:run(name,*args) for name,args in scenarios.items()}
pd.concat(frames.values()).to_csv(P/'mars_tharsis_4Ga_timeseries.csv',index=False)
summary=[]
for name,f in frames.items():
 r=f.iloc[(f.age_Ga-3.85).abs().argmin()];n=f.iloc[-1]
 # Partial-pressure fractions are NOT mole fractions: compute mole fraction using molecular masses.
 h2_mole=(r.H2_atm_bar/2)/(r.CO2_atm_bar/44+r.H2_atm_bar/2+r.H2O_vapor_atm_bar/18) if r.total_atm_bar>0 else 0
 summary.append(dict(scenario=name,CO2_outgassed_4Ga_to_now_bar=n.CO2_outgassed_bar,
 H2O_outgassed_GEL_m=n.H2O_outgassed_GEL_m,H2_outgassed_bar=n.H2_outgassed_bar,
 CO2_3p85Ga_bar=r.CO2_atm_bar,H2_3p85Ga_bar=r.H2_atm_bar,H2O_vapor_3p85Ga_bar=r.H2O_vapor_atm_bar,
 total_3p85Ga_bar=r.total_atm_bar,H2_mole_fraction_3p85Ga=h2_mole,
 CO2_escaped_total_bar=n.CO2_escaped_bar,CO2_sequestered_total_bar=n.CO2_sequestered_bar,
 H2_escaped_total_bar=n.H2_escaped_bar,water_escaped_total_GEL_m=n.H2O_lost_GEL_m,
 reaches_3bar_at_3p85Ga=bool(r.total_atm_bar>=3)))
sumdf=pd.DataFrame(summary);sumdf.to_csv(P/'mars_tharsis_4Ga_summary.csv',index=False)

fig=make_subplots(rows=2,cols=2,subplot_titles=['Atmospheric gas partial pressures','Cumulative CO₂ released and removed',
 'Solar EUV versus bolometric luminosity','Surface water reservoir and cumulative budget'],specs=[[{},{}],[{'secondary_y':True},{}]],vertical_spacing=.22,horizontal_spacing=.12)
blocks=[]
for ix,(name,f) in enumerate(frames.items()):
 start=len(fig.data);visible=ix==0;x=f.age_Ga
 def trace(y,label,color,row,col,dash='solid',secondary=False):
  fig.add_trace(go.Scatter(x=x,y=y,name=label,line=dict(color=color,width=2.4,dash=dash),visible=visible,
       hovertemplate='%{x:.2f} Ga: %{y:.4f}<extra>'+label+'</extra>'),row=row,col=col,secondary_y=secondary if (row,col)==(2,1) else None)
 trace(f.CO2_atm_bar,'CO₂ atmospheric (bar)','#2864a5',1,1)
 trace(f.H2_atm_bar,'H₂ atmospheric (bar)','#d28c21',1,1)
 trace(f.H2O_vapor_atm_bar,'H₂O vapor (bar)','#1a9a97',1,1)
 trace(f.CO2_outgassed_bar,'CO₂ outgassed (bar)','#2864a5',1,2)
 trace(f.CO2_escaped_bar,'CO₂ escaped (bar)','#bd4851',1,2,'dash')
 trace(f.CO2_sequestered_bar,'CO₂ crustal sink (bar)','#888888',1,2,'dot')
 trace(f.solar_EUV_vs_today,'Solar EUV / today','#bd4851',2,1)
 trace(f.solar_luminosity_vs_today,'Solar luminosity / today','#b18b24',2,1,secondary=True)
 trace(f.H2O_outgassed_GEL_m,'H₂O outgassed (m GEL)','#2864a5',2,2)
 trace(f.water_reservoir_GEL_m,'H₂O remaining (m GEL)','#1a9a97',2,2)
 trace(f.H2O_lost_GEL_m,'H₂O escaped (m GEL)','#bd4851',2,2,'dash')
 blocks.append(list(range(start,len(fig.data))))
buttons=[]
for name,idx in zip(frames,blocks):
 vis=[False]*len(fig.data)
 for i in idx:vis[i]=True
 buttons.append(dict(label=name,method='update',args=[{'visible':vis},{'title':f'Mars 4.0 Ga–present | {name}'}]))
fig.update_layout(title='Mars 4.0 Ga–present | Literature-scale post-4Ga',template='plotly_white',height=940,width=1480,
 margin=dict(l=70,r=65,t=170,b=115),legend=dict(orientation='h',x=0,y=-.15),
 updatemenus=[dict(buttons=buttons,x=.01,y=1.15,xanchor='left',yanchor='top')],hovermode='x unified')
for row in [1,2]:
 for col in [1,2]:
  fig.update_xaxes(range=[4,0],title_text='Age before present (Ga)',row=row,col=col)
  fig.add_vrect(x0=3.5,x1=2.0,fillcolor='rgba(205,60,60,0.06)',line_width=0,row=row,col=col)
  fig.add_vline(x=3.0,line=dict(color='rgba(195,40,40,.55)',width=2),row=row,col=col)
  fig.add_vline(x=3.7,line=dict(color='rgba(85,85,85,.5)',width=1,dash='dash'),row=row,col=col)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Partial pressure (bar)',row=1,col=1)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Pressure-equivalent (bar)',row=1,col=2)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='EUV / present',row=2,col=1,secondary_y=False)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Luminosity / present',row=2,col=1,secondary_y=True)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Global equivalent layer (m)',row=2,col=2)
fig.add_annotation(x=.98,y=1.085,xref='paper',yref='paper',text='Red: assumed major-outgassing decline ~3.0 Ga (range 3.5–2.0)',showarrow=False,font=dict(size=12,color='#b63b3b'))
fig.add_annotation(x=.98,y=1.045,xref='paper',yref='paper',text='Grey dashed: illustrative dynamo cessation 3.7 Ga (uncertain)',showarrow=False,font=dict(size=11,color='#666'))
# Epoch boundaries, grey arrow labels in white margin of plot
for ga,lab in [(3.7,'Noachian → Hesperian'),(3.,'Hesperian → Amazonian')]:
 fig.add_annotation(x=ga,y=1.01,xref='x',yref='paper',text=lab,showarrow=True,arrowhead=2,ax=0,ay=-27,
                    font=dict(size=10,color='#777'),arrowcolor='#999')
fig.write_html(P/'mars_tharsis_4Ga_interactive.html',include_plotlyjs=True,full_html=True)

# static 4-panel overview
f=frames['Literature-scale post-4Ga'];plt.rcParams.update({'font.size':10})
fig2,axs=plt.subplots(2,2,figsize=(14,9),layout='constrained')
ax=axs[0,0]
for col,lab,color in [('CO2_atm_bar','CO₂','#2864a5'),('H2_atm_bar','H₂','#d28c21'),('H2O_vapor_atm_bar','H₂O vapor','#1a9a97')]:ax.plot(age,f[col],label=lab,color=color)
ax.set_ylabel('Partial pressure (bar)');ax.legend()
ax=axs[0,1];ax.plot(age,f.CO2_outgassed_bar,label='CO₂ outgassed');ax.plot(age,f.CO2_escaped_bar,label='CO₂ escaped',ls='--');ax.plot(age,f.CO2_sequestered_bar,label='CO₂ sequestered',ls=':');ax.set_ylabel('Cumulative CO₂ (bar)');ax.legend()
ax=axs[1,0];ax.plot(age,eu,label='EUV / present',color='#bd4851');ax.set_ylabel('EUV / present');ax2=ax.twinx();ax2.plot(age,lum,label='Bolometric luminosity',color='#b18b24');ax2.set_ylabel('Luminosity / present')
ax=axs[1,1];ax.plot(age,f.H2O_outgassed_GEL_m,label='Outgassed');ax.plot(age,f.water_reservoir_GEL_m,label='Remaining');ax.plot(age,f.H2O_lost_GEL_m,label='Escaped',ls='--');ax.set_ylabel('H₂O GEL (m)');ax.legend()
for ax in axs.flat:
 ax.set_xlim(4,0);ax.set_xlabel('Age before present (Ga)');ax.grid(alpha=.18);ax.axvspan(3.5,2,color='red',alpha=.035);ax.axvline(3,color='#bd4851',alpha=.55);ax.axvline(3.7,color='grey',ls='--',alpha=.6)
fig2.suptitle('Tharsis-like outgassing versus atmospheric escape | exploratory reference scenario',fontsize=14)
fig2.savefig(P/'mars_tharsis_4Ga_overview.png',dpi=180)
print(sumdf.to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.5f}'))
print('Files generated.')


================================================================================
research/crater_atmosphere/Fresh_Crater_Atmosphere_and_Impact_Revision/original_inputs/fresh_original/mars_fresh_only_inversion.py
================================================================================
"""FRESH-ONLY Bayesian inversion: atmospheric pressure, gas mixture, impactor class.
Uses only Archer (2023) Table 4 fresh-crater elevation-bin means.
No degraded craters, erosion, valley networks, or climate-model priors enter likelihood.
NO independent meteorite classification is possible from these data.
No fragmentation/ablation hydrocode: strength is used only as a survival filter.
Run: python mars_impactor_class_joint.py
"""
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np, pandas as pd
import matplotlib; matplotlib.use('Agg')
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from scipy.special import logsumexp
from scipy.stats import qmc,norm
from sklearn.decomposition import PCA
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler
ROOT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
raw=pd.read_csv(ROOT/'mars_crater_elevation_data.csv')
classes=['2-3','3-4','4-5']; mids=np.array([2.5,3.5,4.5]); z=np.sort(raw.elevation_km.unique())
def matrix(col):
 return np.array([raw[raw.diameter_bin_km.eq(c)].set_index('elevation_km').reindex(z)[col].to_numpy(float) for c in classes])
fresh=matrix('fresh_depth_km')
fsd=matrix('fresh_sd_km')
SF=np.sqrt((fsd/np.sqrt(20))**2+.02**2)
pressures=[.5,1.,1.5,2.,3.]; h2s=[0.,.01,.03,.05,.07,.10]; rhs=[.35,.75]
# Candidate physical *analogs*, not meteorite spectral classifications.
meteorites={
 'Carbonaceous chondrite':(1800,900,0.8),
 'Ordinary chondrite':(3400,5e6,1.0),
 'Stony-iron':(4700,2e7,1.0),
 'Iron':(7800,1e8,1.0)}
# strength [Pa] are broad nominal survival thresholds, not measured Noachian materials.
N=64; u=np.clip(qmc.Sobol(d=10,scramble=True,seed=3852023).random_base2(6),1e-9,1-1e-9)
g=3.71; T=np.clip(270+12*norm.ppf(u[:,0]),235,300)
angle=np.arcsin(np.sqrt(.08+.9*u[:,1])); density_factor=np.exp(.18*norm.ppf(u[:,2]))
relief_ratio=np.exp(np.log(.105)+.10*norm.ppf(u[:,3])); diameter_scale=np.exp(.12*norm.ppf(u[:,4]))
# Size distribution: differential dN/dD~D^-3.5; integrated logarithmic bins ~D^-2.5.
D=np.geomspace(35,1400,75)[None,:,None]
V=np.array([7000.,10000.,13000.,17000.,22000.])[None,None,:]
weights=(D/100)**(-2.5)*np.array([.12,.25,.29,.22,.12])[None,None,:]
weights=np.broadcast_to(weights,(1,75,5)); weights=weights/weights.sum()
def vapor_pressure(t):
 c=t-273.15
 return 611.21*np.exp((18.678-c/234.5)*(c/(257.14+c)))
records=[]; per_bin=[]
for p in pressures:
 for h in h2s:
  for rh in rhs:
   water=np.minimum(.015,rh*vapor_pressure(T)/(p*1e5)); co2=1-h-water
   R=co2*188.9+h*4124+water*461.5; H=R*T/g
   for name,(density,strength,cd) in meteorites.items():
    initial=np.full((N,3,len(z)),np.nan); survival=np.zeros((N,3,len(z)))
    rho_i=density*density_factor
    for k,alt in enumerate(z):
     column=p*1e5*np.exp(-alt*1000/H)/g
     beta=(2/3)*rho_i[:,None,None]*D/cd
     v=V*np.exp(-column[:,None,None]/(2*beta*np.sin(angle[:,None,None])))
     # Approximate fragmentation flag: dynamic pressure at max atmospheric density
     # is NOT a validated breakup model. Reduced weight, rather than hard exclusion.
     rho_air=p*1e5*np.exp(-alt*1000/H)/(R*T)
     q=.5*rho_air[:,None,None]*v*v
     survive=np.exp(-np.maximum(q/strength-1,0)*.7)
     crater=1.3*1.16*diameter_scale[:,None,None]*(rho_i[:,None,None]/3000)**(1/3)*D**.78*(v*v/g)**.22/1000
     for j,(lo,hi) in enumerate([(2,3),(3,4),(4,5)]):
      ww=weights*survive*((crater>=lo)&(crater<hi))
      normw=ww.sum(axis=(1,2))
      dmean=np.divide((ww*crater).sum(axis=(1,2)),normw,out=np.full(N,np.nan),where=normw>0)
      vmean=np.divide((ww*v).sum(axis=(1,2)),normw,out=np.full(N,np.nan),where=normw>0)
      initial[:,j,k]=relief_ratio*dmean*(vmean/12000)**.15
      survival[:,j,k]=normw
    residual_f=((initial-fresh[None,:,:])/SF[None,:,:])**2
    lf=-.5*np.sum(residual_f,axis=(1,2))
    lf[~np.isfinite(initial).all(axis=(1,2))]=-np.inf
    evidence=logsumexp(lf)-np.log(N)
    records.append(dict(pressure_bar=p,h2_pct=100*h,h2o_pct=100*water.mean(),co2_pct=100*co2.mean(),
       relative_humidity=rh,meteorite_class=name,log_evidence=evidence))
    for j,crater_bin in enumerate(classes):
      lfj=-.5*np.sum(residual_f[:,j,:],axis=1)
      lfj[~np.isfinite(initial[:,j,:]).all(axis=1)]=-np.inf
      per_bin.append(dict(pressure_bar=p,h2_pct=100*h,relative_humidity=rh,meteorite_class=name,
          crater_bin_km=crater_bin,log_evidence=logsumexp(lfj)-np.log(N)))
res=pd.DataFrame(records); res['posterior']=np.exp(res.log_evidence-logsumexp(res.log_evidence))
res.to_csv(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_joint_grid.csv',index=False)
summary=[]
for col in ['pressure_bar','meteorite_class','h2_pct','relative_humidity']:
 tab=res.groupby(col,as_index=False).posterior.sum().rename(columns={'posterior':'probability'})
 tab.to_csv(ROOT/f'mars_fresh_only_posterior_{col}.csv',index=False)
 summary.append((col,tab))
binres=pd.DataFrame(per_bin)
for crater_bin,part in binres.groupby('crater_bin_km'):
 part=part.copy();part['posterior']=np.exp(part.log_evidence-logsumexp(part.log_evidence))
 binres.loc[part.index,'within_bin_posterior']=part.posterior
bybin=binres.groupby(['crater_bin_km','meteorite_class'],as_index=False).within_bin_posterior.sum()
bybin.to_csv(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_class_by_crater_bin.csv',index=False)
# PCA includes pressure, impactor density, and gas composition; gases sum to one.
X=res[['pressure_bar','co2_pct','h2_pct','h2o_pct']].copy()
X['impactor_density_kg_m3']=res.meteorite_class.map({k:v[0] for k,v in meteorites.items()})
scaler=StandardScaler();scaled=scaler.fit_transform(X)
pca=PCA(n_components=4).fit(scaled)
load=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=X.columns,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(4)])
load.to_csv(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_pca_loadings.csv')
pd.DataFrame({'component':[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(4)],'variance_fraction':pca.explained_variance_ratio_}).to_csv(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_pca_variance.csv',index=False)
fig,axs=plt.subplots(2,2,figsize=(12,9),layout='constrained')
pr=dict(summary)['pressure_bar'];axs[0,0].bar(pr.pressure_bar.astype(str),pr.probability,color='#477eac');axs[0,0].set(title='Pressure posterior',xlabel='bar',ylabel='Probability')
cl=dict(summary)['meteorite_class'];axs[0,1].barh(cl.meteorite_class,cl.probability,color='#788d79');axs[0,1].set(title='Impactor-class posterior',xlabel='Probability')
pivot=bybin.pivot(index='crater_bin_km',columns='meteorite_class',values='within_bin_posterior').loc[classes]
pivot.plot(kind='bar',ax=axs[1,0]);axs[1,0].set(title='Class ranking by final crater diameter',xlabel='Final crater diameter (km)',ylabel='Within-bin probability');axs[1,0].legend(fontsize=7)
axs[1,1].bar(np.arange(4)+1,pca.explained_variance_ratio_,color='#7c87a6');axs[1,1].set(title='PCA of pressure, gases and impactor density',xlabel='Principal component',ylabel='Variance explained')
fig.suptitle('Fresh-crater-only inversion: atmospheric pressure and impactor classes\nClass priors and simplified survival law strongly affect rankings',fontsize=12)
fig.savefig(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_joint_figure.png',dpi=180,bbox_inches='tight');plt.close(fig)
with open(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_methodology.txt','w') as f:
 f.write('EXPLORATORY MODEL, NOT METEORITE IDENTIFICATION\n\n')
 f.write('Uses ONLY Archer (2023) Table 4 fresh crater relief elevation-bin means.\n')
 f.write('NO degraded crater data, NO valley networks, NO climate/erosion models enter the likelihood.\n')
 f.write('Equal class, pressure, H2 and humidity grid priors.\n')
 f.write('Crater gravity-regime scaling and impactor survival are simplified; ablation, fragmentation,\n')
 f.write('impactor size-frequency distributions and rock strengths are poorly constrained.\n')
 f.write('Likelihood assumes effective crater count 20 and uncertainty floors 20/30 meters.\n')
 f.write('The highest posterior class is conditional on these assumptions and cannot identify a meteorite class.\n')
 f.write('PCA is descriptive, not causal. Gas fractions sum to 100%, creating dependence.\n')
 f.write('The paper has 2-3, 3-4, 4-5 km bins; no 1-2 km data are present.\n')
print('PRESSURE',dict(summary)['pressure_bar'].to_string(index=False))
print('CLASS',cl.to_string(index=False))
print('BY BIN',bybin.to_string(index=False))
print('PCA',pca.explained_variance_ratio_)


================================================================================
research/crater_atmosphere/Fresh_Crater_Atmosphere_and_Impact_Revision/original_inputs/Mars_3p85Ga_Fresh_Craters_ONLY_Atmosphere_Impactor_Bayesian_PCA/Mars_3p85Ga_Fresh_Craters_ONLY_Atmosphere_Impactor_Bayesian_PCA/mars_fresh_only_inversion.py
================================================================================
"""FRESH-ONLY Bayesian inversion: atmospheric pressure, gas mixture, impactor class.
Uses only Archer (2023) Table 4 fresh-crater elevation-bin means.
No degraded craters, erosion, valley networks, or climate-model priors enter likelihood.
NO independent meteorite classification is possible from these data.
No fragmentation/ablation hydrocode: strength is used only as a survival filter.
Run: python mars_impactor_class_joint.py
"""
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np, pandas as pd
import matplotlib; matplotlib.use('Agg')
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from scipy.special import logsumexp
from scipy.stats import qmc,norm
from sklearn.decomposition import PCA
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler
ROOT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
raw=pd.read_csv(ROOT/'mars_crater_elevation_data.csv')
classes=['2-3','3-4','4-5']; mids=np.array([2.5,3.5,4.5]); z=np.sort(raw.elevation_km.unique())
def matrix(col):
 return np.array([raw[raw.diameter_bin_km.eq(c)].set_index('elevation_km').reindex(z)[col].to_numpy(float) for c in classes])
fresh=matrix('fresh_depth_km')
fsd=matrix('fresh_sd_km')
SF=np.sqrt((fsd/np.sqrt(20))**2+.02**2)
pressures=[.5,1.,1.5,2.,3.]; h2s=[0.,.01,.03,.05,.07,.10]; rhs=[.35,.75]
# Candidate physical *analogs*, not meteorite spectral classifications.
meteorites={
 'Carbonaceous chondrite':(1800,900,0.8),
 'Ordinary chondrite':(3400,5e6,1.0),
 'Stony-iron':(4700,2e7,1.0),
 'Iron':(7800,1e8,1.0)}
# strength [Pa] are broad nominal survival thresholds, not measured Noachian materials.
N=64; u=np.clip(qmc.Sobol(d=10,scramble=True,seed=3852023).random_base2(6),1e-9,1-1e-9)
g=3.71; T=np.clip(270+12*norm.ppf(u[:,0]),235,300)
angle=np.arcsin(np.sqrt(.08+.9*u[:,1])); density_factor=np.exp(.18*norm.ppf(u[:,2]))
relief_ratio=np.exp(np.log(.105)+.10*norm.ppf(u[:,3])); diameter_scale=np.exp(.12*norm.ppf(u[:,4]))
# Size distribution: differential dN/dD~D^-3.5; integrated logarithmic bins ~D^-2.5.
D=np.geomspace(35,1400,75)[None,:,None]
V=np.array([7000.,10000.,13000.,17000.,22000.])[None,None,:]
weights=(D/100)**(-2.5)*np.array([.12,.25,.29,.22,.12])[None,None,:]
weights=np.broadcast_to(weights,(1,75,5)); weights=weights/weights.sum()
def vapor_pressure(t):
 c=t-273.15
 return 611.21*np.exp((18.678-c/234.5)*(c/(257.14+c)))
records=[]; per_bin=[]
for p in pressures:
 for h in h2s:
  for rh in rhs:
   water=np.minimum(.015,rh*vapor_pressure(T)/(p*1e5)); co2=1-h-water
   R=co2*188.9+h*4124+water*461.5; H=R*T/g
   for name,(density,strength,cd) in meteorites.items():
    initial=np.full((N,3,len(z)),np.nan); survival=np.zeros((N,3,len(z)))
    rho_i=density*density_factor
    for k,alt in enumerate(z):
     column=p*1e5*np.exp(-alt*1000/H)/g
     beta=(2/3)*rho_i[:,None,None]*D/cd
     v=V*np.exp(-column[:,None,None]/(2*beta*np.sin(angle[:,None,None])))
     # Approximate fragmentation flag: dynamic pressure at max atmospheric density
     # is NOT a validated breakup model. Reduced weight, rather than hard exclusion.
     rho_air=p*1e5*np.exp(-alt*1000/H)/(R*T)
     q=.5*rho_air[:,None,None]*v*v
     survive=np.exp(-np.maximum(q/strength-1,0)*.7)
     crater=1.3*1.16*diameter_scale[:,None,None]*(rho_i[:,None,None]/3000)**(1/3)*D**.78*(v*v/g)**.22/1000
     for j,(lo,hi) in enumerate([(2,3),(3,4),(4,5)]):
      ww=weights*survive*((crater>=lo)&(crater<hi))
      normw=ww.sum(axis=(1,2))
      dmean=np.divide((ww*crater).sum(axis=(1,2)),normw,out=np.full(N,np.nan),where=normw>0)
      vmean=np.divide((ww*v).sum(axis=(1,2)),normw,out=np.full(N,np.nan),where=normw>0)
      initial[:,j,k]=relief_ratio*dmean*(vmean/12000)**.15
      survival[:,j,k]=normw
    residual_f=((initial-fresh[None,:,:])/SF[None,:,:])**2
    lf=-.5*np.sum(residual_f,axis=(1,2))
    lf[~np.isfinite(initial).all(axis=(1,2))]=-np.inf
    evidence=logsumexp(lf)-np.log(N)
    records.append(dict(pressure_bar=p,h2_pct=100*h,h2o_pct=100*water.mean(),co2_pct=100*co2.mean(),
       relative_humidity=rh,meteorite_class=name,log_evidence=evidence))
    for j,crater_bin in enumerate(classes):
      lfj=-.5*np.sum(residual_f[:,j,:],axis=1)
      lfj[~np.isfinite(initial[:,j,:]).all(axis=1)]=-np.inf
      per_bin.append(dict(pressure_bar=p,h2_pct=100*h,relative_humidity=rh,meteorite_class=name,
          crater_bin_km=crater_bin,log_evidence=logsumexp(lfj)-np.log(N)))
res=pd.DataFrame(records); res['posterior']=np.exp(res.log_evidence-logsumexp(res.log_evidence))
res.to_csv(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_joint_grid.csv',index=False)
summary=[]
for col in ['pressure_bar','meteorite_class','h2_pct','relative_humidity']:
 tab=res.groupby(col,as_index=False).posterior.sum().rename(columns={'posterior':'probability'})
 tab.to_csv(ROOT/f'mars_fresh_only_posterior_{col}.csv',index=False)
 summary.append((col,tab))
binres=pd.DataFrame(per_bin)
for crater_bin,part in binres.groupby('crater_bin_km'):
 part=part.copy();part['posterior']=np.exp(part.log_evidence-logsumexp(part.log_evidence))
 binres.loc[part.index,'within_bin_posterior']=part.posterior
bybin=binres.groupby(['crater_bin_km','meteorite_class'],as_index=False).within_bin_posterior.sum()
bybin.to_csv(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_class_by_crater_bin.csv',index=False)
# PCA includes pressure, impactor density, and gas composition; gases sum to one.
X=res[['pressure_bar','co2_pct','h2_pct','h2o_pct']].copy()
X['impactor_density_kg_m3']=res.meteorite_class.map({k:v[0] for k,v in meteorites.items()})
scaler=StandardScaler();scaled=scaler.fit_transform(X)
pca=PCA(n_components=4).fit(scaled)
load=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=X.columns,columns=[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(4)])
load.to_csv(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_pca_loadings.csv')
pd.DataFrame({'component':[f'PC{i+1}' for i in range(4)],'variance_fraction':pca.explained_variance_ratio_}).to_csv(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_pca_variance.csv',index=False)
fig,axs=plt.subplots(2,2,figsize=(12,9),layout='constrained')
pr=dict(summary)['pressure_bar'];axs[0,0].bar(pr.pressure_bar.astype(str),pr.probability,color='#477eac');axs[0,0].set(title='Pressure posterior',xlabel='bar',ylabel='Probability')
cl=dict(summary)['meteorite_class'];axs[0,1].barh(cl.meteorite_class,cl.probability,color='#788d79');axs[0,1].set(title='Impactor-class posterior',xlabel='Probability')
pivot=bybin.pivot(index='crater_bin_km',columns='meteorite_class',values='within_bin_posterior').loc[classes]
pivot.plot(kind='bar',ax=axs[1,0]);axs[1,0].set(title='Class ranking by final crater diameter',xlabel='Final crater diameter (km)',ylabel='Within-bin probability');axs[1,0].legend(fontsize=7)
axs[1,1].bar(np.arange(4)+1,pca.explained_variance_ratio_,color='#7c87a6');axs[1,1].set(title='PCA of pressure, gases and impactor density',xlabel='Principal component',ylabel='Variance explained')
fig.suptitle('Fresh-crater-only inversion: atmospheric pressure and impactor classes\nClass priors and simplified survival law strongly affect rankings',fontsize=12)
fig.savefig(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_joint_figure.png',dpi=180,bbox_inches='tight');plt.close(fig)
with open(ROOT/'mars_fresh_only_methodology.txt','w') as f:
 f.write('EXPLORATORY MODEL, NOT METEORITE IDENTIFICATION\n\n')
 f.write('Uses ONLY Archer (2023) Table 4 fresh crater relief elevation-bin means.\n')
 f.write('NO degraded crater data, NO valley networks, NO climate/erosion models enter the likelihood.\n')
 f.write('Equal class, pressure, H2 and humidity grid priors.\n')
 f.write('Crater gravity-regime scaling and impactor survival are simplified; ablation, fragmentation,\n')
 f.write('impactor size-frequency distributions and rock strengths are poorly constrained.\n')
 f.write('Likelihood assumes effective crater count 20 and uncertainty floors 20/30 meters.\n')
 f.write('The highest posterior class is conditional on these assumptions and cannot identify a meteorite class.\n')
 f.write('PCA is descriptive, not causal. Gas fractions sum to 100%, creating dependence.\n')
 f.write('The paper has 2-3, 3-4, 4-5 km bins; no 1-2 km data are present.\n')
print('PRESSURE',dict(summary)['pressure_bar'].to_string(index=False))
print('CLASS',cl.to_string(index=False))
print('BY BIN',bybin.to_string(index=False))
print('PCA',pca.explained_variance_ratio_)


================================================================================
research/crater_atmosphere/Fresh_Crater_Atmosphere_and_Impact_Revision/original_inputs/Mars_Tharsis_4Ga_Outgassing_Escape_Interactive_Model/Mars_Tharsis_4Ga_Outgassing_Escape/mars_tharsis_model.py
================================================================================
"""Scenario-based, not uniquely calibrated, Tharsis-like volatile outgassing model.
Requires numpy pandas plotly matplotlib. Produces interactive HTML, PNG and CSV.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np,pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import plotly.graph_objects as go
from plotly.subplots import make_subplots
P=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
age=np.linspace(4,0,801);dt=.005;eu=(4.57/(4.57-age))**1.23;lum=1/(1+.4*age/4.57)
scenarios={'Literature-scale post-4Ga':(.35,22,.3,0),'Generous Tharsis':(1.5,65,1.,0),'Extreme 3-bar cumulative':(3.,130,2.,0),'Inherited 2.5-bar reservoir':(.35,22,.3,2.5)}

def run(name,co2_total,water_total,h2_water_ratio,initial):
 w=np.exp(-.5*((age-3.83)/.24)**2)+.25*np.exp(-.5*((age-3.27)/.4)**2)+.02*np.exp(-.5*((age-2.3)/.5)**2)
 w/=np.sum(w)*dt
 co2_source=co2_total*w; water_source=water_total*w
 h2_source=water_source*.0371*h2_water_ratio*(2/18)
 c=np.zeros(len(age));h=np.zeros(len(age));v=np.zeros(len(age));water=np.zeros(len(age)); c[0]=initial
 esc_c=np.zeros(len(age));esc_h=np.zeros(len(age));esc_w=np.zeros(len(age));fixed=np.zeros(len(age))
 for i in range(1,len(age)):
  j=i-1;shield=.65 if age[j]>3.7 else 1.
  loss_c=.022*eu[j]**.65*shield*c[j]; mineral=.024*c[j]
  tau=.012/np.sqrt(eu[j]/3)
  loss_h=h[j]/tau
  loss_w=.18*eu[j]**.7 if water[j]>0 else 0
  c[i]=max(0,c[j]+dt*(co2_source[j]-loss_c-mineral))
  h[i]=max(0,h[j]+dt*(h2_source[j]-loss_h))
  water[i]=max(0,water[j]+dt*(water_source[j]-loss_w))
  # Atmospheric vapor is saturation-limited; water inventory is distinct.
  temp=265+6*(lum[j]-.75)/.25
  saturation=.00611*np.exp(17.27*(temp-273.15)/(temp-35.85))
  v[i]=min(water[i]*.0371,.65*saturation)
  esc_c[i]=esc_c[j]+dt*loss_c;esc_h[i]=esc_h[j]+dt*loss_h;esc_w[i]=esc_w[j]+dt*loss_w;fixed[i]=fixed[j]+dt*mineral
 return pd.DataFrame(dict(scenario=name,age_Ga=age,CO2_atm_bar=c,H2_atm_bar=h,H2O_vapor_atm_bar=v,
  total_atm_bar=c+h+v,water_reservoir_GEL_m=water,CO2_outgassed_bar=np.cumsum(co2_source)*dt,
  H2_outgassed_bar=np.cumsum(h2_source)*dt,H2O_outgassed_GEL_m=np.cumsum(water_source)*dt,
  CO2_escaped_bar=esc_c,H2_escaped_bar=esc_h,H2O_lost_GEL_m=esc_w,CO2_sequestered_bar=fixed,
  CO2_source_bar_per_Gyr=co2_source,H2_source_bar_per_Gyr=h2_source,H2O_source_GEL_m_per_Gyr=water_source,
  solar_EUV_vs_today=eu,solar_luminosity_vs_today=lum))

frames={name:run(name,*args) for name,args in scenarios.items()}
pd.concat(frames.values()).to_csv(P/'mars_tharsis_4Ga_timeseries.csv',index=False)
summary=[]
for name,f in frames.items():
 r=f.iloc[(f.age_Ga-3.85).abs().argmin()];n=f.iloc[-1]
 # Partial-pressure fractions are NOT mole fractions: compute mole fraction using molecular masses.
 h2_mole=(r.H2_atm_bar/2)/(r.CO2_atm_bar/44+r.H2_atm_bar/2+r.H2O_vapor_atm_bar/18) if r.total_atm_bar>0 else 0
 summary.append(dict(scenario=name,CO2_outgassed_4Ga_to_now_bar=n.CO2_outgassed_bar,
 H2O_outgassed_GEL_m=n.H2O_outgassed_GEL_m,H2_outgassed_bar=n.H2_outgassed_bar,
 CO2_3p85Ga_bar=r.CO2_atm_bar,H2_3p85Ga_bar=r.H2_atm_bar,H2O_vapor_3p85Ga_bar=r.H2O_vapor_atm_bar,
 total_3p85Ga_bar=r.total_atm_bar,H2_mole_fraction_3p85Ga=h2_mole,
 CO2_escaped_total_bar=n.CO2_escaped_bar,CO2_sequestered_total_bar=n.CO2_sequestered_bar,
 H2_escaped_total_bar=n.H2_escaped_bar,water_escaped_total_GEL_m=n.H2O_lost_GEL_m,
 reaches_3bar_at_3p85Ga=bool(r.total_atm_bar>=3)))
sumdf=pd.DataFrame(summary);sumdf.to_csv(P/'mars_tharsis_4Ga_summary.csv',index=False)

fig=make_subplots(rows=2,cols=2,subplot_titles=['Atmospheric gas partial pressures','Cumulative CO₂ released and removed',
 'Solar EUV versus bolometric luminosity','Surface water reservoir and cumulative budget'],specs=[[{},{}],[{'secondary_y':True},{}]],vertical_spacing=.22,horizontal_spacing=.12)
blocks=[]
for ix,(name,f) in enumerate(frames.items()):
 start=len(fig.data);visible=ix==0;x=f.age_Ga
 def trace(y,label,color,row,col,dash='solid',secondary=False):
  fig.add_trace(go.Scatter(x=x,y=y,name=label,line=dict(color=color,width=2.4,dash=dash),visible=visible,
       hovertemplate='%{x:.2f} Ga: %{y:.4f}<extra>'+label+'</extra>'),row=row,col=col,secondary_y=secondary if (row,col)==(2,1) else None)
 trace(f.CO2_atm_bar,'CO₂ atmospheric (bar)','#2864a5',1,1)
 trace(f.H2_atm_bar,'H₂ atmospheric (bar)','#d28c21',1,1)
 trace(f.H2O_vapor_atm_bar,'H₂O vapor (bar)','#1a9a97',1,1)
 trace(f.CO2_outgassed_bar,'CO₂ outgassed (bar)','#2864a5',1,2)
 trace(f.CO2_escaped_bar,'CO₂ escaped (bar)','#bd4851',1,2,'dash')
 trace(f.CO2_sequestered_bar,'CO₂ crustal sink (bar)','#888888',1,2,'dot')
 trace(f.solar_EUV_vs_today,'Solar EUV / today','#bd4851',2,1)
 trace(f.solar_luminosity_vs_today,'Solar luminosity / today','#b18b24',2,1,secondary=True)
 trace(f.H2O_outgassed_GEL_m,'H₂O outgassed (m GEL)','#2864a5',2,2)
 trace(f.water_reservoir_GEL_m,'H₂O remaining (m GEL)','#1a9a97',2,2)
 trace(f.H2O_lost_GEL_m,'H₂O escaped (m GEL)','#bd4851',2,2,'dash')
 blocks.append(list(range(start,len(fig.data))))
buttons=[]
for name,idx in zip(frames,blocks):
 vis=[False]*len(fig.data)
 for i in idx:vis[i]=True
 buttons.append(dict(label=name,method='update',args=[{'visible':vis},{'title':f'Mars 4.0 Ga–present | {name}'}]))
fig.update_layout(title='Mars 4.0 Ga–present | Literature-scale post-4Ga',template='plotly_white',height=940,width=1480,
 margin=dict(l=70,r=65,t=170,b=115),legend=dict(orientation='h',x=0,y=-.15),
 updatemenus=[dict(buttons=buttons,x=.01,y=1.15,xanchor='left',yanchor='top')],hovermode='x unified')
for row in [1,2]:
 for col in [1,2]:
  fig.update_xaxes(range=[4,0],title_text='Age before present (Ga)',row=row,col=col)
  fig.add_vrect(x0=3.5,x1=2.0,fillcolor='rgba(205,60,60,0.06)',line_width=0,row=row,col=col)
  fig.add_vline(x=3.0,line=dict(color='rgba(195,40,40,.55)',width=2),row=row,col=col)
  fig.add_vline(x=3.7,line=dict(color='rgba(85,85,85,.5)',width=1,dash='dash'),row=row,col=col)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Partial pressure (bar)',row=1,col=1)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Pressure-equivalent (bar)',row=1,col=2)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='EUV / present',row=2,col=1,secondary_y=False)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Luminosity / present',row=2,col=1,secondary_y=True)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Global equivalent layer (m)',row=2,col=2)
fig.add_annotation(x=.98,y=1.085,xref='paper',yref='paper',text='Red: assumed major-outgassing decline ~3.0 Ga (range 3.5–2.0)',showarrow=False,font=dict(size=12,color='#b63b3b'))
fig.add_annotation(x=.98,y=1.045,xref='paper',yref='paper',text='Grey dashed: illustrative dynamo cessation 3.7 Ga (uncertain)',showarrow=False,font=dict(size=11,color='#666'))
# Epoch boundaries, grey arrow labels in white margin of plot
for ga,lab in [(3.7,'Noachian → Hesperian'),(3.,'Hesperian → Amazonian')]:
 fig.add_annotation(x=ga,y=1.01,xref='x',yref='paper',text=lab,showarrow=True,arrowhead=2,ax=0,ay=-27,
                    font=dict(size=10,color='#777'),arrowcolor='#999')
fig.write_html(P/'mars_tharsis_4Ga_interactive.html',include_plotlyjs=True,full_html=True)

# static 4-panel overview
f=frames['Literature-scale post-4Ga'];plt.rcParams.update({'font.size':10})
fig2,axs=plt.subplots(2,2,figsize=(14,9),layout='constrained')
ax=axs[0,0]
for col,lab,color in [('CO2_atm_bar','CO₂','#2864a5'),('H2_atm_bar','H₂','#d28c21'),('H2O_vapor_atm_bar','H₂O vapor','#1a9a97')]:ax.plot(age,f[col],label=lab,color=color)
ax.set_ylabel('Partial pressure (bar)');ax.legend()
ax=axs[0,1];ax.plot(age,f.CO2_outgassed_bar,label='CO₂ outgassed');ax.plot(age,f.CO2_escaped_bar,label='CO₂ escaped',ls='--');ax.plot(age,f.CO2_sequestered_bar,label='CO₂ sequestered',ls=':');ax.set_ylabel('Cumulative CO₂ (bar)');ax.legend()
ax=axs[1,0];ax.plot(age,eu,label='EUV / present',color='#bd4851');ax.set_ylabel('EUV / present');ax2=ax.twinx();ax2.plot(age,lum,label='Bolometric luminosity',color='#b18b24');ax2.set_ylabel('Luminosity / present')
ax=axs[1,1];ax.plot(age,f.H2O_outgassed_GEL_m,label='Outgassed');ax.plot(age,f.water_reservoir_GEL_m,label='Remaining');ax.plot(age,f.H2O_lost_GEL_m,label='Escaped',ls='--');ax.set_ylabel('H₂O GEL (m)');ax.legend()
for ax in axs.flat:
 ax.set_xlim(4,0);ax.set_xlabel('Age before present (Ga)');ax.grid(alpha=.18);ax.axvspan(3.5,2,color='red',alpha=.035);ax.axvline(3,color='#bd4851',alpha=.55);ax.axvline(3.7,color='grey',ls='--',alpha=.6)
fig2.suptitle('Tharsis-like outgassing versus atmospheric escape | exploratory reference scenario',fontsize=14)
fig2.savefig(P/'mars_tharsis_4Ga_overview.png',dpi=180)
print(sumdf.to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.5f}'))
print('Files generated.')
